P5879: **EX-8. Current closed-session target helper - EA 1893-1930.** The whole `UjClosedSessionTarget` body that 15.4 replaces.
P5880:
P5881: ```mql5
P5882:  1893: bool UjClosedSessionTarget(const SManagedTrade &t, const int barShift, double &px)
P5883:  1894:    {
P5884:  1895:     px = 0.0;
P5885:  1896:     if(t.dir != DIR_LONG && t.dir != DIR_SHORT) return false;
P5886:  1897:     int es = t.sessionAtEntry;
P5887:  1898:     if(es != SESSION_LONDON && es != SESSION_NYAM) return false;
P5888:  1899:     if(t.uj_admitBarTime <= 0) return false;
P5889:  1900:      datetime bt = iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, barShift);
P5890:  1901:      if(bt == 0) return false;
P5891:  1902:      if((int)CurrentTradingWindow(bt) == es) return false;
P5892:  1903:      datetime bp = iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, barShift + 1);
P5893:  1904:      if(bp == 0) return false;
P5894:  1905:      if((int)CurrentTradingWindow(bp) != es) return false;  // first-out-bar pin (V347 Luna-2): barShift must be the first bar outside the entry-session run
P5895:  1906:      double ext = 0.0;
P5896:  1907:     bool have = false;
P5897:  1908:     bool uj_ended = false;
P5898:  1909:     datetime uj_newest = 0;
P5899:  1910:     datetime uj_oldest = 0;
P5900:  1911:      for(int k = barShift + 1; k < barShift + 601; k++)
P5901:  1912:        {
P5902:  1913:         datetime bk = iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, k);
P5903:  1914:         if(bk == 0) break;
P5904:  1915:         if((int)CurrentTradingWindow(bk) != es)
P5905:  1916:           {
P5906:  1917:            if(have) uj_ended = true;
P5907:  1918:            break;  // contiguous-run end (V347 Luna-2): the walk starts inside an es run by the barShift+1 pin; admission containment is the separate P097 test, so any non-es bar ends the walk - fail-closed when no price seen yet
P5908:  1919:           }
P5909:  1920:        if(uj_newest == 0) { uj_newest = bk; uj_oldest = bk; }
P5910:  1921:        else uj_oldest = bk;
P5911:  1922:        double v = (t.dir == DIR_LONG) ? iHigh(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, k) : iLow(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, k);
P5912:  1923:        if(v <= 0.0) continue;
P5913:  1924:        if(!have || (t.dir == DIR_LONG && v > ext) || (t.dir == DIR_SHORT && v < ext)) { ext = v; have = true; }
P5914:  1925:       }
P5915:  1926:     if(!have || !uj_ended || ext <= 0.0) return false;
P5916:  1927:     if(t.uj_admitBarTime < uj_oldest || t.uj_admitBarTime > uj_newest) return false;
P5917:  1928:     px = ext;
P5918:  1929:     return true;
P5919:  1930:    }
P5920: ```

P5922: **EX-9. Eviction readers and writers - EA 8105-8120 and EA 9318-9330.** The two write sites plus their read sites, which 15.5's eviction census depends on.
P5923:
P5924: ```mql5
P5925:  8105:           {
P5926:  8106:            PrintFormat("[SRJ-EA] RESEED_BLOCKED bar=%s action=INDEX-INVALID", TimeToString(iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, barShift), TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES));
P5927:  8107:            return;
P5928:  8108:           }

P5932:  8112:            else if(rsq_bit >= 0 && (g_evictBitsLon & (1 << rsq_bit)) != 0) rsq_blocked = true;
P5933:  8113:           }

P6031: Regions already carried above and presence-asserted rather than re-spliced: EA 349-378 (10.1), EA 10641-10656 (10.2), EA 10608-10634 (10.3), EA 11771-11785 (10.4), EA 11843-11850 (10.5), EA 11913-11938 (10.6), EA 12034-12069 (10.7), EA 7445-7457 (10.8), EA 7457-8069 (10.9), EA 8094-8429 (10.10), EA 8430-8476 (10.11), EA 2337-2378 (11.1), and EA 11815-11825 (12.2).
P6032:
P6033: Two measured caveats, stated rather than passed over:
P6034:
P6035: 1. Four literal three-dot sequences appear inside the spliced regions above, at EA 10860, EA 10862, EA 11148, and EA 11151. They are EA comment and `Print` text (a truncated digest, a converter call written as an ellipsis, and two warning strings), not builder elisions. Zero three-dot sequences occur in any builder-authored sentence of this packet. The regions are otherwise byte-exact, and editing those four lines to remove the dots would falsify the exhibit.
P6036: 2. The inherited exhibits at 10.9 and 10.10 are not byte-identical to the saved EA at twelve lines: EA 7523, 7525, 7556, 7798, 7825, 7855, 7858, 7861 in 10.9 and EA 8189, 8213, 8217, 8219 in 10.10. The cause is an encoding divergence on those EA lines, which carry a double-encoded dash or check glyph while the packet copies show the intended character. Every other line of every inherited region is byte-present. The regions spliced by this fold are byte-exact against disk, verified line by line, which means they reproduce the EA's own encoding exactly - including the three EA comment lines inside EX-1 at EA 8773, EA 8789 and EA 8791 that carry the double-encoded glyph as the file holds it. A reader comparing one of those lines with the inherited exhibits is looking at the encoding divergence above, not at a corruption introduced here. The council should decide whether the inherited twelve lines are re-spliced from disk in the implementation packet.
P6037: ### 15.3 The day-close price-reference defect, source-proven and kept separate
P6038:
P6039: This is a third defect. It is not the June 5 TP_TOUCH management retirement and it is not the RECON57 model-versus-broker close; folding it into either would hide it. It concerns which price the day-close leg books.
P6040:
P6041: Source facts, all on-page in 15.2:
P6042:
P6043: - The day mark is built from 16:55 New York and converted to server time (EA 11009, EX-11). In the RECON78 window that mark lands at 23:55 server.
P6044: - `vDAY` fires on the first evaluated bar at or after the mark (EA 12017, EX-5). The 23:55 bar is therefore evaluated on the following pass, which is exactly why the RECON57 verdict row is stamped 2026.09.07 00:00:07 - Friday's 23:55 bar processed on Monday's first tick.
P6045: - Inside `EvaluateManagedTrade` (EA 11843) the local `nextOpenPx` is the open of the bar BEFORE the evaluated bar, with a fallback to the evaluated bar's close (EA 11857-11858, EX-4).
P6046: - The day-close leg books that local (EA 12043, EX-5).
P6047:
P6048: Consequence: the model books the open of the bar after the 23:55 verdict bar, which is the next day's open, while his pinned reference is the 23:55 verdict-day bar's own open. The next-day-open semantic his 2026-09-25 word retired for DAY_CLOSE is still in the saved EA. A weekday mark books the next session's open; a Friday mark books a weekend-gapped price.
P6049:
P6050: Stale cross-reference found while proving this: the comment at EA 12054 attributes `nextOpenPx` to EA 11290/11292, but those lines are HTFCENSUS print statements. The assignments are at EA 11857-11858; a second, unrelated local of the same name exists at EA 9355-9357. Any seat or builder reading the comment alone would cite the wrong lines, so the comment must be corrected with the fix.
P6051:
P6052: Required disposition, design level: the day-close leg books the open of the verdict bar itself; the verdict row names that bar; the broker close is attempted at that price under the same sync rules as the TP leg. Whether a closed-bar host can act at that price is the trigger-cadence question Sonnet C3 raises, and it is a design decision for this review: the price reference is fixed by his pin and is not negotiable here, while the host cadence and its fallback must be stated before any build. No operator ruling is requested.
P6053:
P6054: RECON57 evidence hygiene, per Sonnet C10 and GLM C1: no RECON57 physical row numbers are cited anywhere in this packet until they are extracted from the RECON57 file itself; deal #7 is paired to #4 by symbol and volume only, deals #5 and #6 are unseen, and no PID is shown for any of them; #7 filled at 1.16307 against a model target of 1.16302, and no close reason is inferred from that; `MtCloseBrokerPosition` (definition EA 11793, call EA 12057) post-dates RECON78, so no run on record shows it executing; the expected reason for an EA-initiated market close is `DEAL_REASON_EXPERT`, to be verified against the MQL5 reference before encoding rather than asserted from memory.
P6055:
P6056: June 11 evidence gap, per Luna, Sonnet B3 and GLM D10: the 14:30 high and low, the 14:35 open, high and low, and the Daily-POC values at bar shifts 1 and 2 are not in the archive. They are not fabricated here and the gap is not a reason to reopen the settled setup. The future run's own confirmation rows are the acceptance evidence; the chart values remain an operator-side item that closes the gap if he supplies them, and nothing in this contract waits on it.
P6057:
P6058: ### 15.4 Q1 contract - closed-session target revisions and day-close management
P6059:
P6060: **15.4.1 Eligible-session table and target evaluation.** The current helper is bound to the entry session only (EA 1898, EX-8), pins the first bar outside that session (EA 1905), walks a contiguous run (EA 1911-1925), and is additionally constrained by admission containment, so a London trade cannot be revised by a later closed New York AM session, and the one-revision-per-instance comment is a further source constraint. The replacement evaluates, for every session that has closed since the entry, the session's own extreme in the trade's direction, and proposes it as a revision when it is nearer than the operative target and valid: on the profit side of entry and ahead of the executable price. Open-session extremes are never eligible. Session closing is detected per session from the same mark machinery the day-close leg uses, not by a bar-count walk. The comparator base is the last confirmed-synchronized revision where one exists, otherwise the broker TP carried at fill; `tpRef` alone is not the base. The validity predicate is the existing nearest-target validity rule, which the current helper does not call: `ComputeNearestTpTarget` (EA 2508) and `MtNearestTpTarget` (EA 11586, called at EA 11900) exist in the saved EA and are the rules that decide whether a candidate line is a valid target. No new post-entry 1R gate is introduced.
P6061:
P6062: **15.4.2 Instance registry.** The singleton managed record (EA 10641-10656, carried at 10.2) is replaced by a PID-keyed registry in execute and tester modes. Registration happens only after a confirmed fill, so a failed entry leaves no instance. A later signal never overwrites, resets, or retires an open instance; the current replacement-and-reset boundary (EA 10645-10656) and `MtReset` (EA 349-378, carried at 10.1) may not run against a live PID. Retirement is defined only after broker PID resolution, that is after a full-volume `DEAL_ENTRY_OUT` for that PID is read from history, or after a bounded history lookup fails and the instance is published as an orphan still awaiting broker resolution. In alert-only mode there is no broker PID, so the paper instance is keyed by candidate sequence plus admission bar, and any change to paper behavior must be diffed against the seven valid EURUSD takes before it is accepted.
P6063:
P6064: **15.4.3 State machine and transition table.** Liveness authority is the broker PID, not the model's own close flag. Model `TP_TOUCH` never retires an open PID. The `MT_CLOSED` write (EA 12037, carried at 10.7) and the state gate that returns on every later pass (EA 11845-11846, carried at 10.5) are exactly what made the 5 June day mark unreachable while the broker position lived; both are superseded. The full transition table the seats asked for:
P6065:
P6066: | # | State or event | Next | Binding notes |
P6067: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
P6068: | 1 | Fill in execute mode | OPEN; PID registered after confirmed fill; revision 0 equals the broker TP and is SYNCED | no instance on a failed entry |
P6069: | 2 | Eligible nearer closed-session target | revision n PENDING_SYNC; UJRETARGET printed unconditionally; first attempt on the same pass | a new revision cancels the previous revision's pending budget |
P6070: | 3 | Modify DONE with exact TP readback equal to revision n and unchanged SL | SYNCED(n) | one sync row per revision, never per bar |
P6071: | 4 | Retryable failure (TIMEOUT, CONNECTION, PRICE_CHANGED, TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, REQUOTE) | PENDING; at most three actual calls, at most one per M5 bar | MARKET_CLOSED is a visible deferral and spends no call |
P6072: | 5 | Permanent failure, unknown retcode, or the third failure | SYNC_FAILED(n); one EXIT-UNSYNCED row per revision | original SL and TP stay in place; no market close |
P6073: | 6 | Target already passed, or inside stops or freeze distance | no broker call; revision marked unaccepted | no market close and no forced exit; see 15.4.6 |
P6074: | 7 | NO_CHANGES | SKIP only when the readback equals the revision; otherwise a failed attempt | a skip is not a call and spends no budget |
P6075: | 8 | Model touch while SYNCED | AWAIT_DEAL; MODEL_ONLY when no deal follows | the instance stays live |
P6076: | 9 | Model touch while PENDING or SYNC_FAILED | EXIT-UNSYNCED row and alert | the instance stays live and masks no other leg |
P6077: | 10 | PID absent with a full-volume DEAL_ENTRY_OUT, TP or SL reason | RESOLVED; instance retired | a TP deal at the last-synced revision is valid; it becomes stale only after a newer revision confirms |
P6078: | 11 | BREAK | market close, at most three calls, one per eligible bar | success is DONE with a same-PID OUT deal and a flat position |
P6079: | 12 | HTF | non-executing; the instance stays live | HTF-off must be asserted at acceptance |
P6080: | 13 | DAY_CLOSE | as row 11 | price reference per 15.3; trigger cadence stated before any build |
P6081: | 14 | Close pending | CLOSE_PENDING; still managed | |
P6082: | 15 | Close terminal failure | CLOSE_FAILED, visible, still managed | other legs keep evaluating |
P6083: | 16 | PID absent with a history lookup | resolve the deal; else UJORPHAN or EXIT_PENDING_BROKER; a partial fill stays live | history-lookup retries are bounded and the bound is stated in the implementation packet |
P6084:
P6085: Same-bar precedence is SL, then the confirmed-synchronized TP, then BREAK, then HTF, then DAY_CLOSE. An unsynchronized TP never outranks another winning leg.
P6086:
P6087: Model touch versus broker fill is a distinction, not a defect class: the model evaluates its own chart series, so a SHORT's model touch is a touch of the chart low while the broker fills that side on the ask, and with a live spread the fill can follow the touch or never happen at all. MODEL_ONLY is therefore a normal SHORT outcome under rows 8 and 9 above, and the acceptance test is always the broker deal, never the model print. No separate operator rule is invented for this; the operator's pins already fix the exact target price and forbid leniency, and the classification follows from the model-versus-broker separation.
P6088:
P6089: **15.4.4 Broker TP synchronization and supersession.** The sync helper named by GLM C6 does not exist in the saved EA (no `SyncBrokerTp` occurrence on disk); it is new code. Sync is proven only by a documented success retcode plus the same PID or ticket plus an exact normalized TP readback equal to the revision plus an unchanged normalized SL. A Boolean success is never treated as execution. Supersession is stated as the seats require: the old broker TP becomes SUPERSEDED only after the new revision's readback succeeds; before that it remains the operative target. That resolves the contradiction Sonnet C4 identified, by separating classification from acceptance - a fill at the last-synced target is a valid EA outcome, but it never satisfies TP-branch acceptance for a revision whose own sync was not confirmed. Retcode numeric meanings are NOT binding from memory: the table in the earlier sections is carried as a proposal, and every class must be verified against the current MQL5 reference before encoding, as both Luna and Sonnet require. `CTrade` asynchronous mode must be pinned off, and the ticket overload of `PositionModify`, the history-selection call, and `DEAL_REASON_TP` must be verified in the same pass.
P6090:
P6091: **15.4.5 Verdict flag split.** `vDAY` is computed only when no other verdict is set (EA 12013, EX-5), and BREAK and HTF are gated the same way, so a stale target flag masks the remaining legs. The flag splits into `vTP_synced`, which carries priority, and model-touch telemetry, which never sets a verdict. Every gate that tests the old flag migrates, including the model-close write (EA 12037) and the MTLIFE and EXIT alerts (EA 12061-12068), which must not print for a model-only touch.
P6092:
P6093: **15.4.6 Internal no-modify states.** A target already passed, or inside the stop or freeze distance, is an internal state, never a server retcode. No modify call is issued, the revision is marked unaccepted, the position stays open and stays managed, and no market close is inferred. Per 15.1 the retarget object must be ahead of price to be valid at all, so this state arises only when the session closes with its extreme already behind the position; in that case the next nearest valid target governs. Nothing here exits at market, and no operator ruling is required.
P6094:
P6095: **15.4.7 Modes, O6, phantom instances, sizing.** The broker close helper is guarded by a single line in the saved EA, EA 11795 (EX-12): execute mode AND tester, otherwise SKIP-NO-SEND with a -1 return. The packet therefore names the exact close predicate as it stands, and states plainly that demo is not in the exhibited guard and that a live account never closes from this helper; any demo or live close policy is a separate operator-authority question that this contract does not invent. The concurrency guard `IsSessionPositionOpen` (EA 10756-10757, definition EA 1754-1763, EX-7) must move ahead of the managed-record replacement and reset (EA 10645-10656) and ahead of the signal side effects (EA 10618-10625), for both execute and alert-only modes; a blocked candidate emits no SIGNAL, no A6Fired row, and consumes no session use. The active flag is set only after a confirmed fill, never before the send and never before the concurrency, lot, and stops aborts. Hedging or netting posture, synchronous `CTrade`, ticket overload, and tester and demo policy are pinned before any readback timing is claimed. Sizing is path-dependent: lots derive from equity, so the London exit moving from 159.900 to 159.908 changes equity before the New York entry and every later volume can move; acceptance therefore compares closing volume with that PID's opening volume, and the baseline volumes are comparison values only.
P6096:
P6097: **15.4.8 Day-close fixture specification.** Authored before any run, as GLM C7 requires. Symbol, dates, session, entry bar and side, the day marks in play, the no-eligible-target condition that forces the day-close branch in isolation, and the isolation from any session target that could win first. Monday through Thursday proves the mid-week branch only; Friday acceptance stays unproven until a Friday fixture exists, and the RECON57 model verdict alone proves nothing about a broker close.
P6098:
P6099: TP-branch fixture identity is read from deal history and never from an assumed PID: 5 June London USDJPY SHORT admitted at the 09:40 bar (entry deal number 4, stop reference 159.972) and 5 June New York USDJPY LONG admitted at the 16:50 bar (entry deal number 6, stop reference 159.726). PIDs are resolved from the deal history at grade time because a PID is never assumed to equal an order number. Every required row is unconditional: the retarget print is debug-flag-gated in the saved EA, so acceptance can never depend on that flag. The per-fixture event chain is the retarget row, the modify call with its raw boolean and retcode, the exact target readback with an unchanged stop, and then a same-PID full-volume exit deal with the target reason at exactly the operative revision with no stop or old-target deal earlier. Each of these fails the branch: a deal at the old target or at the stop, a failed modify, a failed readback, a target deal at any other price, or a retcode outside the closed set. The downstream diff starts at 5 June London 12:05 and runs to journal end, classifying every admission, signal, abort, and deal including the equity-driven volume changes that follow the London exit. The New York signal and model entry at 160.115 and the deal at 160.120 stay separate observations. In alert-only mode the paper-only behavior is retained with no modify call and no close call at all.
P6100:
P6101: **15.4.9 HTF.** The model retires while the PID is live if HTF is ever re-enabled, so acceptance records HTF-off. No HTF broker-close policy is invented here; his open experiment stays open and unexercised, and no close policy is inferred for the cancel-bias or replaced-exit reasons.
P6102:
P6103: ### 15.5 Q3 contract - the June 11 LONG through ordinary same-pass admission
P6104:
P6105: **15.5.1 Repaired confirmation predicate.** The current function returns at the prior-close clause (EA 2369, carried whole at 11.1) before it evaluates body and touch, and its touch clause is conjunctive rather than the operator's independent alternative. The repaired predicate is exactly touch-or-break with zero margin and zero tolerance:
P6106:
P6107: | Case | Prior-close clause | Touch of the line | Repaired result |
P6108: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
P6109: | Retest bar touches the line and closes into setup-bias direction | any | yes | CONFIRMED, same bar permitted |
P6110: | Body close through the line in setup-bias direction | any | not required | CONFIRMED, break arm |
P6111: | Neither touch nor body close through the line | any | no | NOT CONFIRMED, with the failing term named |
P6112: | Bar before the retest closed through the line | fails | any | prior close is irrelevant; it never vetoes |
P6113:
P6114: Bar mapping is unchanged for unrelated consumers: the confirmation bar is the bar that carries the retest plus confirmation, and it is evaluated on the next pass, so the June 11 case is the 14:35 bar evaluated at the 14:40 pass with the entry at the 14:40 open. The repair changes the prior-close arm only, and every other consumer of the function keeps its current bar mapping.
P6115:
P6116: Caller census, complete, from the saved EA: thirteen call sites. Ex-10 carries each with context. BUILDER-PROPOSED classification, for council to confirm or correct: live gates are 8337, 8360, 8367 (S1 candidate and its two direction branches), 9052 (carry gate), 9067 and 9246 (S5 pass and confirm); the F11 holder test at 7449, the UJOPCONF probes at 7874 and 7875, the contender probes at 8438 and 8439, and the seed probe at 8268 are holder or telemetry paths whose classification the council must settle, because each one changes a hold, displace, or yield decision rather than a promotion; 8242 is an N1 counter probe that gates nothing. Because a relaxed confirmation is the highest-risk edit for keeping invalid setups silent, the implementation packet must carry an old-versus-new diff at every one of the thirteen sites across the full journal, with every flipped result classified against the register.
P6117:
P6118: **15.5.2 Same-pass state map.** The current release path is terminal: `GoAbort` ends by setting the abort state and calling `ResetSequence` (EA 6630-6633, EX-6), and `ResetSequence` clears the state, direction, anchor line, anchor price, anchor bar time, divergence latch, touch state, and alert-armed flag (EA 6571-6600, EX-6). The fresh LONG must reach the ordinary pipeline inside the 14:35 pass without a second bar pass and without losing fresh state, so the release sequence is specified as:
P6119:
P6120: 1. The deferred abort that matches the SHORT holder is CONSUMED, not applied: one UJDEFERCONSUME row, one STAND-DOWN row, no UJDEFERAPPLY, and no holder-held row for the LONG. The yield alternative is one UJDEFERDROP plus one SIDE1C_YIELD and no consume. The two are mutually exclusive on one bar.
P6121: 2. Holder-scoped state written this bar is cleared exactly once before the fresh candidate is processed: `s1_stopRef`, `s1_haveStop`, the `uj_memo_*` family, the SHORT-keyed `g_divLatch`, the confirmation and freshness locals, and the `g_ujOpReseed*` and `s1g_*` carriers. The clear runs inside the release, never as a second pass.
P6122: 3. Seed-bar locals are recomputed for the released candidate, because `s1f_seedArmed` is computed before the IDLE gate (EA 8070 against EA 8072, EX-1) and on the release bar the state is not IDLE at that point; without the recompute the seed-bar telemetry never fires, including the SIDE1 rows the acceptance relies on.
P6123: 4. Poll and memo availability in the same pass: the fresh candidate's memo and poll are computed scoped to that candidate's identity before the seed, so no holder-scoped memo or latch is read and no prepass rerun is introduced.
P6124: 5. The fresh candidate then runs the ordinary S1, S2, S3, S4 and S5 path once, with the confirmation predicate of 15.5.1.
P6125:
P6126: **15.5.3 Return census with per-return abort disposition.** Every early return between the incumbent phase and the resolver carries an explicit disposition of consume, drop, or retain. Seed-region returns at EA 8074, 8090, 8093, 8107, 8125; S1 returns at EA 8391, 8395, 8397; S2 returns at EA 8408, 8421, 8423; plus the S1WAIT and S2WAIT returns. The deferred-abort apply sits outside the S3/S4 block and after the S1WAIT and S2WAIT returns, so an abort set for an S1 or S2 holder is retained across those returns today; the new contract requires each of those returns to state whether a pending abort survives it. No return may be left silent.
P6127:
P6128: **15.5.4 Eviction and provenance census.** The read key is derived from the top line and direction per session and day, and the two write sites are spliced at EX-9. The requirement is that every write uses the same key formula, so a consumed SHORT Daily-POC identity cannot evict or block the LONG Daily-POC identity; the implementation packet lists every eviction write with its session, day, line, and direction key and every provenance field with its writer, so the claim is checkable rather than asserted. A flip-killed holder never vetoes a challenger, and a live position of the session blocks every new setup; these two rules govern different states and are not merged.
P6129:
P6130: **15.5.5 Counters and suppression.** Every counter is keyed by bar time, phase, and candidate identity, with one accounting path per key and mutually exclusive yield, preempt, reseed, supersede, abort, consume, and early-return classes. The pre-decision suppression census must print a neutral, read-only label on the release bar rather than a held action, or be moved after resolution. Suppression itself is neutral and read-only: it never consumes an abort, a session use, or a signal.
P6131:
P6132: **15.5.6 Exact acceptance and preservation.** The registered identity is 11 June, New York, USDJPY, LONG, Daily-POC. Required: the 14:35 retest plus confirmation through normal fresh initialization under the repaired predicate; the signal and model entry reference exactly the 14:40 open at 160.524; the actual deal recorded separately with its spread delta; no spread tolerance, no relabeling, no rounding; no selection evidence from 14:45 or later; confirmation proof rows for the LONG at 14:35 with the chart values where his chart supplies them; LTF alignment true for the fresh candidate after the flip kill, otherwise the run ends in the seed-bias kill or S2WAIT and that cause must be classified. Any divergence between the reference and the deal is a defect under no-leniency, reported and never waived. A refusal by any normal guard is an implementation defect against the settled setup, not authority to bypass the guard.
P6133:
P6134: Preservation controls, all binding: 5 June New York USDJPY silent on its observed seed-bias kill; 9 June never-reseeded refusal preserved; 5 June London USDJPY 09:45 SHORT at 159.948 preserved; 3 June 09:10 preserved; the seven valid EURUSD takes preserved; one take per pair, session, and day with cross-session independence; exactly one accepted June 11 take scoped by pair, session, date, anchor, and direction rather than a run-wide count; and the full-journal old-versus-new confirmation diff plus the downstream diff after Q1 for equity, lot shifts, session use, and evict state.
P6135:
P6136: Ordering: acceptance of the June 11 New York LONG requires the earlier June 5 New York position to be broker-closed by the Q1 TP leg first. If that PID is still open, O6 must block the June 11 candidate, and that block is correct behavior rather than a defect.
P6137:
P6138: ### 15.6 Provenance and grading boundaries
P6139:
P6140: The saved EA source, its EX5, and the RECON78 run are bound separately and their linkage stays unproven until exhibited: a digest match on the source does not prove which bytes ran, and a tester PASS does not prove a broker modification or a broker day-close close. The intended tree for any future implementation is the EA digest recorded in this packet's status header, and the implementation packet must re-measure it before any build. Acceptance uses future-run rows only; nothing in this review claims that a modification, a revised-target deal, or a broker day-close close occurred, and no June 11 alert, admission, or deal exists in the baseline.
